d-randy-thom/)\nciteturn73search8 [wordlim: 200] Published: 5.3 years ago; Crawled: last month; I began talking with Steve and Julia pretty early about the sound for that film, and they’re kind of natural sound gatherers anyway, so I didn’t really need to tell them this, but one thing I always tell documentary makers is to just record as much sound as you can even if the camera’s not rolling, just record everything that you can record because recording space is cheap and you just never know what you might pick up that will be useful in the cut.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nA Deep Dive Into the Hidden Audio Tools of FCPX - Frame.io Insider (https://blog.frame.io/2018/08/27/deep-dive-fcpx-audio-tools/)\nciteturn73search9 [wordlim: 200] Published: 8.2 years ago; Crawled: 2 weeks ago; \n\n### For synchronized clips\n\nIn the case of synchronized clips, this is as easy as selecting the clip and going to our trusty CLIP → OPEN CLIP function. Once the clip is opened in the timeline, you can slip the sync manually by dragging and/or nudging the audio with your keyboard shortcuts\n\nNudge left 1 frame: , (comma)\nNudge right 1 frame: . (period)\n\nYou can also make adjustments to your sync directly in the multicam clip. Double click on your multicam clip to open it in the timeline. Then click on the triangle disclose on the upper left of each angle. There you will find additional options, such as setting one angle or another as the Monitoring Angle. This is the angle to which all the other angles will be synced. The best candidate is the one that contains the longest unbroken clip.\n\nImage: Screen Shot 2018 08 07 at 4.22.40 PM\n\nYou can also use this menu to re-sync a clip that came in slightly off during the first pass. It will be synced to the newly assigned Monitoring Angle by selecting it and choosing Sync Selection To Monitoring Angle from the dropdown.\n\n### Batch-syncing\n\nPersonally, I like to sync my footage in Resolve, if for no other reason than it allows batch-syncing and Final Cut does not. Also FCPX does not provide Checksum Verify (a feature which ensures your footage is being copied from the camera card to your hard drive properly) so I like to use Resolve for initial camera card download and syncing for this reason as well.\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nBehind the Scenes of the Hot Musical Doc \"Summer of Soul\" (https://blog.frame.io/2021/09/22/art-of-the-cut-summer-of-soul/)\nciteturn73search10 [wordlim: 200] Published: 5.1 years ago; Crawled: last month; Talk to me about why the documentary was started like that.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nDive into \"Death on the Nile\" and \"Belfast\" with editor Úna ní Dhonghaíle (https://blog.frame.io/2022/04/06/art-of-the-cut-special-belfast-and-death-on-the-nile-editor-una-ni-dhongaile/)\nciteturn73search11 [wordlim: 200] Published: 4.6 years ago; Crawled: last month; I think as the editor, we have to learn that diplomatic skill that the director is on set, and our job day-to-day is to check if there is anything missing. ... Do this as many times as needed with multiple asset versions to combine all within a single thumbnail location. Be sure the new version has fully uploaded before creating your Version Stack.\nL36: ​\nL37: Once you've created the Version Stack, double-click to open in the Player Page, the newest version will be ready to view.\nL38: ​\nL39: Access all of the versions by clicking the version number at the top of the page. You can view any previous version in that stack.\nL40: You can also compare versions in the player page in a special side-by-side viewer. 【11†Click here to learn more.】\nL41: ## Reorder versions within your version stack\nL42: \nL43: On the project page, click the menu options on your version stack by clicking the three-dot menu and select Version History. Drag and drop the versions into the proper order.\nL44: ​\nL45: To quickly manage the version stack, you can click on the version icon on the asset from within the project page.\nL46: ​\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nInternal Error ()\nciteturn73view2 [wordlim: 200] Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://mediaworkhouse.com/studio-hire/podcast-studio/\",\"lineno\":120}); \nFailed to fetch https://mediaworkhouse.com/studio-hire/podcast-studio/: (400) Timeout fetching--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nInternal Error ()\nciteturn73view3 [wordlim: 200] Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://www.synology.com/en-us/dsm/7.2/software_spec/ai_console\",\"lineno\":null}); \nFailed to fetch https://www.synology.com/en-us/dsm/7.2/software_spec/ai_console: (400) Timeout fetching"}]

[Input: Search for answers] [Input] [Input]\nL18: \nL19:   * There are no suggestions because the search field is empty.\nL20: \nL21: # Trint MCP Connector\nL22: ## Learn all about the Trint MCP Connector, how to use it and how it functions within your workflow.\nL23: \nL24: An MCP server is an open standard that lets AI agents and assistants access external tools and data sources. The Trint MCP Connector uses this open standard, so that journalists, researchers, and media teams already working with Claude or another AI assistant, can bring Trint’s capabilities directly into their workflows, without switching context.\nL25: This is an implementation of Anthropic’s Model Context Protocol (MCP) that makes Trint’s capabilities available to any MCP-compatible AI tool. Trint is not just a standalone app, but a tool inside your AI workflow.\nL26: \nL27: This support guide will cover:\nL28: \nL29:   * 【5†What does the Trint MCP Connector do】\nL30:   * 【6†Why we've created it】\nL31:   * 【7†How to set up the Trint MCP Connector】\nL32:   * 【8†FAQs】\nL33: ### What does the Trint MCP Connector do?\nL34: \nL35: The Trint MCP Connector exposes a set of tools that a connected AI agent can call on the user’s behalf. These tools allow the AI to:\nL36: \nL37:   * Get transcript content – Retrieve the full text of any Trint file the user has access to\nL38: \nL39:   * Search – Query across a Trint workspace or specific folder to find relevant files or moments\nL40: \nL41:   * Find quotes – Identify the best quotes according to a prompt the user provides.\nL42:   * View and play quotes – Render a quote within an agent and playback. Open a quote on 【9†app.trint.com†app.trint.com】 to edit and comment.\nL43:   * Export – Download a completed transcript or selected moments as edl, srt and docx\nL44: \nL45: Together, these tools enable an end-to-end workflow: transcribe - find - extract - draft - export - entirely within an AI assistant or agentic pipeline.\nL46: \nL47: Keep your eyes peeled for even more updates on the Trint MCP Connector, as we roll out more features and improvements.\nL48: ### Why have we created a Trint MCP Connector?\nL49: \nL50: General purpose AI tools can do some of the functions of Trint, such as transcribe, summarise, find key quotes. But there are some things they can’t do. And because Trint is an AI tool workflow built for media production and newsrooms, we’re able to take what works in Trint and amplify it with the MCP Connector.\nL51: \nL52: Here's an example of when the Trint MCP Connector is most beneficial:\nL53: You’ve got a quote from a speaker that you’re checking and verifying. With the Trint MCP Connector you can get the audio of the quote along with the response from the AI agent so that you can double check the transcript was correct while hearing the soundbite out loud, making sure it’s right for your content.\nL54: \nL55: 【10†Image】\nL56: \nL57: And every quote always has a mapping link back to the original transcript so you can easily open it up and work with it in the Trint UI.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nRelease Update - Summer 2026 (https://info.trint.com/knowledge/release-update-summer-2026)\nciteturn74view1 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 2 days ago; Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://info.trint.com/knowledge/release-update-summer-2026\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 117\nL0: 【0†iframe†www.googletagmanager.com】\nL1: \nL2: 【1†Skip to content】\nL3: \nL4: English Show submenu for translations\nL5: \nL6: 【2†Customer portal】\nL7: \nL8: Open main navigation Close main navigation\nL9: \nL10:   * English Show submenu for translations\nL11: \nL12:   * 【3†Customer portal】\nL13:   * 【4†Go to trint.com†trint.com】\nL14: \nL15: 【4†Go to trint.com†trint.com】\nL16: \nL17: Looking to do more with Trint? [Input: Search for answers] [Input] [Input]\nL18: \nL19:   * There are no suggestions because the search field is empty.\nL20: \nL21: # Release Update - Summer 2026\nL22: ## Read about Trint’s latest bug fixes, improvements and new features recently added to Trint across April, May and June 2026.\nL23: \nL24: 【5†Release Update - April 2026】\nL25: \nL26: 【6†Release Update - May 2026】\nL27: \nL28: 【7†Release Update - June 2026】\nL29: \nL30: ### Release Update - April 2026\nL31: \nL32: #### New ✨\nL33: ##### AI Highlights are here!\nL34: \nL35: AI Highlighting is now a part of the AI Assistant, there to source and define key moments so you don’t have to. It works in two modes – live and pre-recorded – meaning teams can get highlights in seconds, ready to turn into content.\nL36: \nL37: Recent improvements to the AI Highlights feature include:\nL38:   * The quotes produced from the feature are less frequent and more selective, using a prompt tuned by our VP of Product’s domain knowledge.\nL39:   * The tool is now triggered on the end of a sentence as well as at the end of paragraph.\nL40:   * The triggering logic has been optimised, so it is now triggering every 15 seconds, meaning highlights are set to be updated more often, avoiding long delays before the first highlight generated.\nL41: For more information about AI Highlights, please see our 【8†support guide here】 or speak to your Customer Success Manager.\nL42: ####\nL43: \nL44: #### Improved 👩‍💻\nL45: \nL46: ##### Trint ASR version 1.29 release\nL47: \nL48: We now have a build version of our ASR Batch image which is better at handling segments and less likely to miss chunks of transcripts.\nL49: ##### Desktop App now uses H264 codec for live streaming\nL50: \nL51: The Desktop App was using an old FLV1 video codec which produces high bitrate compared to the standard RTMP codec H264. The FLV1 codec also doesn't allow for constant bitrate and framerate control, whereas the H264 codec will have fixed framerate and will downscale the video to 480p.\nL52: #### Bugs 🐞\nL53:   * We fixed a bug in the transcript watcher API that prevented messages going to a dlq when errors occurred and also cleaned up the part of the code that caused the issue.\nL54:   * We’ve patched up the server that handles our RTMP push streams to clean up memory when a stream finishes.\nL55:   * We fixed a bug that could set some transcript states to null during secure deletions, which prevented unicorns UI from rendering properly, and stopped us from easily getting visibility of the outcome of the deletions.\nL56: * * *\nL57: ### Release Update - May 2026\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nHow to Set up the Trint MCP Connector (https://info.trint.com/knowledge/how-to-set-up-the-trint-mcp-connector)\nciteturn74view2 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: 3 days ago; Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://info.trint.com/knowledge/how-to-set-up-the-trint-mcp-connector\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 134\nL0: 【0†iframe†www.googletagmanager.com】\nL1: \nL2: 【1†Skip to content】\nL3: \nL4: English Show submenu for translations\nL5: \nL6: 【2†Customer portal】\nL7: \nL8: Open main navigation Close main navigation\nL9: \nL10:   * English Show submenu for translations\nL11: \nL12:   * 【3†Customer portal】\nL13:   * 【4†Go to trint.com†trint.com】\nL14: \nL15: 【4†Go to trint.com†trint.com】\nL16: \nL17: Looking to do more with Trint? [Input: Search for answers] [Input] [Input]\nL18: \nL19:   * There are no suggestions because the search field is empty.\nL20: \nL21: # How to Set up the Trint MCP Connector\nL22: ## Find out how to set up the Trint MCP Connector. Follow our step-by-step guide to connecting your AI agent to Trint.\nL23: \nL24: Most AI tools will give you a summary, pull some quotes, and move on. What they won't tell you is whether the quote is accurate, who actually said it, or where in the recording it came from.\nL25: \nL26: The Trint MCP Connector lets AI assistants like Claude and ChatGPT access your Trint account securely and safely.\nL27: An MCP (Model Context Protocol) server is an open standard that lets AI agents and assistants access external tools and data sources. The Trint MCP Connector uses this open standard, so that journalists, researchers, and media teams already working with Claude or another AI assistant, can bring Trint’s capabilities directly into their workflows, without switching context.\nL28: \nL29: The AI tool does the work. Trint makes it accurate enough to trust.\nL30: \nL31: \nL32: \nL33: This support guide will cover:\nL34:   * 【5†How to set up the Trint MCP Connector using Claude】\nL35:   * 【6†How to set up the Trint MCP Connector using ChatGPT】\nL36:   * 【7†How to set up the Trint MCP Connector using Gemini】\nL37: \nL38: -----------------\nL39: ### Trint MCP Connector Set Up Guide\nL40: #### Claude (【8†claude.ai†claude.ai】)\nL41: \nL42: Step 1: Open Claude in the Desktop app, if installed on your machine, or online at 【8†claude.ai†claude.ai】 in your web browser. Navigate to the left sidebar and select ‘Customize’.\nL43: \nL44: 【9†Image】\nL45: \nL46: Step 2: Click on ‘Connectors’.\nL47: \nL48: 【10†Image: Claude MCP setup step 2】\nL49: \nL50: Step 3: Navigate to the 'Search connectors' button and type in 'Trint'. You will then see Trint as an option to connect. Choose the correct domain for you and press 'Connect'.\nL51: 【11†Image: Claude MCP Setup step 3】\nL52: \nL53: Step 4: Alternatively, you can choose to click on the 'Browse' button and search 'Trint' in the connectors directory. From here, select the Trint connector.\nL54: \nL55: 【12†Image: Claude MCP Setup step 4】\nL56: \nL57: Step 5: Click the ‘Connect’ button. You will then be prompted to provide authentication via the Trint app.\nL58: \nL59: 【13†Image: Claude MCP setup step 5】\nL60: \nL61: Step 6: Log into Trint as usual.\nL62: \nL63: 【14†Image】\nL64: \nL65: Step 7: Your Trint and Claude are now connected.\nL66: \nL67: 【15†Image】\nL68: Step 8: You will now be able to see Trint in your connectors page. From here you can set permissions for each tool and customize access.\nL69: \nL70: 【16†Image】\nL71: \nL72: Step 9: Once your permissions have been set, you can then toggle the Trint Connector on and off in the chat page.\nL73: \nL74: 【17†Image】\nL75: \nL76: Step 10: From here, you are ready to use the Connector. Write your prompt in Claude and reference the Trints you want to use (or your whole Trint library, if you have allowed access) and let Claude work.\nL77: \nL78: From keyword to deep AI semantics — unlock more powerful search as your NAS grows.\nL855: \nL856: 【321†Download Now】【322†Explore AI Mode 7.1.0】\nL857: ## What is Qsirch?\nL858: \nL859: Qsirch is QNAP's dedicated NAS search engine. Its intuitive interface lets you switch between standard search (keyword / AI semantic) and RAG Search, quickly finding files stored on your NAS or accessing advanced data analysis.\nL860: \nL861:   * 【323†Image: AI Knowledge Search (RAG Search)】\nL862: ### AI Knowledge Search (RAG Search) NEW\nL863: \nL864: Combines Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and LLM technology. All inference runs locally on your NAS.\nL873: \nL874: 【322†Learn More】\nL875: \nL876:   * 【326†Image: AI Semantic Search】\nL877: ### AI Semantic Search\nL878: \nL879: Deeply understands the semantics and context of your query to deliver faster, more precise image search results.\nL880: \nL881: 【327†Learn More】\nL882: \nL883:   * 【328†Image: Keyword Search】\nL884: \nL885: ### Keyword Search\nL886: \nL887: Instantly find documents, images, videos, music, and emails by keyword — blazing fast.\nL888: \nL889: 【329†Learn More】\nL890: \nL891: NEW\nL892: ## AI Knowledge Search (RAG Search) Beta :\nL893: AI-Powered Analysis Search, Build Your NAS Knowledge Base\nL894: \nL895: Qsirch combines cloud LLMs and RAG technology to support AI-driven natural language search, delivering accurate information and insights. Deploy On-prem RAG for complete enterprise privacy.\nL896: \nL897: RAG Search supports Word, Excel, PPT, PDF, Email (.eml), and TXT file formats.\nL898: \nL899: 【330†Learn More】\nL900: \nL901: 【331†Image: Qsirch RAG Search】\nL902: \nL903: New in 7.1.0 AI Mode\nL904: ## One sentence. Search every media format.\nL905: \nL906: Powered by a dual-core LLM × VLM AI engine, search documents, images, videos, and audio simultaneously — all AI inference runs locally on your NAS with zero data leakage risk.\nL907: \nL908: Requires a QNAP NAS (x74 series or above) with a compatible NVIDIA or Intel discrete GPU (16GB VRAM or more). 【332†View Compatibility List】\nL909: \nL910:   * 【333†Image】\nL911: ### AI Summary\nL912: \nL913: Generate full summaries of documents, images, videos, and audio directly in search results — no need to open files.\nL914: \nL915:   * 【334†Image】\nL916: \nL917: ### Document Semantic Search\nL918: \nL919: Pinpoint the most relevant paragraphs inside PDF, DOCX, PPTX, TXT, MD — no keywords needed, no file opening required.\nL920: \nL921:   * 【335†Image】\nL922: \nL923: ### Image Semantic Search\nL924: \nL925: Describe what you're looking for in plain language. No filename memorization, no manual tagging — AI reads the photo directly.\nL926: \nL927:   * 【336†Image】\nL928: ### Video Semantic Search\nL929: \nL930: Type a description to jump directly to the key moment in a video. Bidirectional transcript sync — no scrubbing.\nL931: \nL932:   * 【337†Image】\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nGet started with Claude Cowork | Claude Help Center (https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13345190-get-started-with-claude-cowork)\nciteturn74view5 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13345190-get-started-with-claude-cowork\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 334\nL0: 【0†Skip to main content】\nL1: \nL2: 【1†API docs†docs.claude.com】【2†Release notes】【3†How to get support】\nL3: \nL4: [Select]\nL5: \nL6: English\nL7: \nL8: Search`⌘K`\nL9: \nL10:   * 【4†Claude】\nL11: \nL12:   * 【5†Pro, Max, and Ultra plans】\nL13: \nL14:   * 【6†Team and Enterprise plans】\nL15: \nL16:   * 【7†Identity management (SSO, JIT, SCIM)】\nL17: \nL18:   * 【8†Claude Cowork】\nL19: \nL20:   * 【9†Claude Code】\nL21: \nL22:   * 【10†Claude Desktop】\nL23: \nL24:   * 【11†Claude Mobile apps】\nL25: \nL26:   * 【12†Claude API and Console】\nL27: \nL28:   * 【13†Connectors】\nL29: \nL30:   * 【14†Claude in Chrome】\nL31: \nL32:   * 【15†Claude for Education】\nL33: \nL34:   * 【16†Privacy and legal】\nL35:   * 【17†Safeguards】\nL36: \nL37:   * 【18†Amazon Bedrock】\nL38: # Get started with Claude Cowork\nL39: \nL40: Updated yesterday\nL41: \nL42: Copy for LLM\nL43: \nL44: This article explains how to use 【19†Claude Cowork†claude.com】, which brings Claude Code's agentic capabilities to knowledge work beyond coding.\nL45: ## Availability\nL46: \nL47: Claude Cowork is available on paid plans (Pro, Max, Ultra, Team, and Enterprise):\nL48: \nL49:   * Claude Desktop for macOS, Windows, and Linux (beta): Available on all paid plans. Download or update at 【20†claude.com/download†claude.com】.\nL50: \nL51:   * Web and Claude Mobile: Pro, Max, Ultra, and Team plans (in beta on Team), and Enterprise plans when an owner enables it.\nL52:   * Claude in Chrome side panel: All paid plans. On Enterprise plans, an owner needs to enable Claude in Chrome and Cowork in the cloud. See 【21†Get started with Claude in Chrome】 for more information.\nL53: The new Claude experience, which combines chat and Cowork, is rolling out to Pro, Max, and Ultra plans. On Team plans (in beta) and Enterprise plans, it's available where Chat and Cowork unified is turned on. If your message box no longer shows \"Chat\" and \"Cowork\" options, you have the new experience, and some steps in this article may look different. Learn more in 【22†Claude Cowork and chat are one Claude】.\nL54: On desktop, web, and mobile, chat and Cowork share one home, so you start both from the same place. Find the message box and select \"Cowork,\" then describe your task. To go back to a regular conversation, select \"Chat.\" In the Chrome side panel, opening the panel starts a Cowork session directly.\nL55: \nL56: * * *\nL57: ## What is Claude Cowork?\nL58: \nL59: Claude Cowork uses the same agentic architecture that powers Claude Code, with no terminal required. Instead of responding to prompts one at a time, Claude can take on complex, multi-step tasks and execute them on your behalf.\nL60: With Cowork, you can describe an outcome, step away, and come back to finished work—formatted documents, organized files, synthesized research, and more. Cowork runs your sessions remotely in the cloud, so your sessions and files live with your Claude account and follow you across desktop, web, and mobile. Chat and Cowork now share one home, so handing Claude a task starts from the same message box as a conversation. With scheduled tasks, Claude can complete work for you automatically.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nUpload & analyze files in Gemini Apps - Computer - Gemini Apps Help (https://support.google.com/gemini/answer/14903178?hl=en)\nciteturn74view6 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://support.google.com/gemini/answer/14903178?hl=en\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 232\nL0: 【0†Gemini Apps Help】\nL1: \nL2: [Input: Describe your issue]\nL3: \nL4: 【1†Sign in†accounts.google.com】\nL5: \nL6: Send feedback on...\nL7: \nL8: [Input]\nL9: \nL10: This help content & information\nL11: \nL12: [Input]\nL13: \nL14: General Help Center experience\nL15: \nL16: [Button: Close][Button: Next]\nL17: \nL18: # Upload & analyze files in Gemini Apps\nL19: \nL20: You can upload documents, spreadsheets, notebooks, photos, videos, and more to Gemini Apps to get answers, summaries, and insights about your content.\nL21: ## What you need\nL22: \nL23:   * If you’re signed in to a work or school Google Account:\nL24:     * To upload files from Google Drive, access to apps in Gemini must be enabled by your organization’s Workspace administrator. 【2†Learn about administrators】.\nL25:     * Learn more about 【3†using Gemini Apps with a work or school account】 or 【4†how to enable access to apps in Gemini Apps】.\nL26: \nL27: To use this feature, you must be signed in to Gemini Apps. 【5†Learn how to sign in to Gemini Apps】.\nL28: Tip: To get higher limits for this feature, like the ability to upload more files or ask Gemini Apps to reference uploaded files in more chats, you can upgrade to a Google AI plan. Learn more 【6†about limits】 and 【7†how to upgrade】.\nL29: ## Upload files & code\nL30: ### In the Gemini web app\nL31:   1. On your computer, go to 【8†gemini.google.com†gemini.google.com】.\nL32:   2. In the text box at the bottom, enter your question or prompt.\nL33:   3. Click Add files 【9†Image†lh3.googleusercontent.com】.\nL34:     1. To upload a supported file from your device: click Upload Files 【10†Image†lh3.googleusercontent.com】.\nL35:     2. To add a code folder: click More Uploads 【11†Image: and then†lh3.googleusercontent.com】 Import Code 【12†Image†lh3.googleusercontent.com】 【11†Image: and then†lh3.googleusercontent.com】 Upload folder.\nL36:     3. To add a GitHub repository: More Uploads 【11†Image: and then†lh3.googleusercontent.com】 Import Code 【12†Image†lh3.googleusercontent.com】 and enter the GitHub repository or branch URL. 【13†Learn more about adding GitHub repositories】.\nL37:     4. To add a supported file from your Google Drive: click Add from Drive 【14†Image†lh3.googleusercontent.com】.\nL38:       1. To add files from your Drive, you need to have 【15†Keep Activity†myactivity.google.com】 on and connect Google Workspace to Gemini Apps. If it’s not connected, you'll get the option to connect it. 【16†Learn about your Gemini Apps and Google Workspace data】.\nL39:     5. To add a notebook: click More Uploads 【11†Image: and then†lh3.googleusercontent.com】 Notebooks 【17†Image: Notebook†lh3.googleusercontent.com】.\nL40:   4. Click Submit 【18†Image†lh3.googleusercontent.com】.\nL41: Tips:\nL42: \nL43:   * You can add up to 10 (subject to availability) of any kind of supported file in the same prompt, such as documents, images, or videos.\nL44:   * If you get an error that Gemini failed to analyze your file correctly, re-upload it.\nL45: \nL46: In Gemini in Chrome\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"chunk_id\":\"cb9a97\",\"wall_time_seconds\":10.0222328,\"session_id\":76801,\"original_token_count\":0,\"output\":\"\"}"}]

Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://workflow.frame.io/guide\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 1449\nL0: 【0†Image】\nL1: \nL2: 01\nL3: \nL4: ##### Chapter 01\nL5: \nL6: # Capture\nL7: \nL8: We dive into camera technology so that you can start your workflow off right, covering topics like cameras, codecs, and color spaces.\nL9: \nL10: 9 minute read\nL11: \nL12: 【1†Image】\nL13: \nL14: The filmmaking process begins long before the cameras start rolling, but that is where our journey starts, as we chart the factors and decisions that you will need to make throughout the post-production process.\nL15: ## How to choose your capture codec\nL16: \nL17: Share\nL18: \nL19: One of the first decisions to make is what codec to use, since most cameras offer many different options for how to record your video.\nL20: \nL21: 【2†Image】\nL22: It’s vital to consider your complete workflow at this point, because the camera and the codec that you choose now will have major implications for your entire workflow. In order to make your final decision about the camera and codec, you’ll need to return to this section after you’ve read the rest of this guide, but we’ll give you a good overview here.\nL23: \nL24: 【3†What is a codec?】\nL25: Generally speaking, you should aim for the highest-quality codec that your camera (or your budget) can capture. When we say “highest quality”, we mean that you want to capture as much information as possible—so you want less compression, higher bit rate, higher bit depth, and less chroma subsampling (click on the box above for more explanations). The more information you have when you capture, the more flexibility you will have later, especially in color-correction and VFX.\nL26: [Check out the 【4†Codec Comparison】 to see for yourself how different codecs impact image quality.]\nL27: \nL28: 【5†Image】\nL29: ### Factor 1: Cost\nL30: The first consideration for many is cost. Generally speaking, the more expensive the camera, the better the available codecs are, but there are some exceptions. There are some “sweet spot” cameras that offer excellent codecs at a reasonable price. Panasonic’s GH series (especially in the early days when the GH2 was hacked) are known for offering better codecs than the other cameras in their price range. (Panasonic’s GH5 can record internal 4:2:2 10-bit recording at 4K for under US$2,000).\nL31: \nL32: 【6†Image】\nL33: Blackmagic Design (along with the Magic Lantern hack for Canon DSLRs) brought raw recording options to lower-budget cameras a few years ago. These cameras are the exceptions, however.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nRetrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) / Open WebUI Generation (RAG)\nL70: \nL71: 【42†Image: Chat & Conversations map with the Documents (RAG) cell highlighted】【43†Image: Chat & Conversations map with the Documents (RAG) cell highlighted】\nL72: \nL73: warning\nL74: If you're using Ollama, note that it picks a model's default context length from your GPU's VRAM, and on GPUs with less than 24 GiB that is 4096 tokens (see 【44†Ollama's context length documentation†docs.ollama.com】). This severely limits Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) performance, especially for web search, because retrieved data may not be used at all or only partially processed.\nL75: Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) is a technique that enhances the conversational capabilities of chatbots by incorporating context from diverse sources. It works by retrieving relevant information from a wide range of sources such as local and remote documents, web content, and even multimedia sources like YouTube videos. The retrieved text is then combined with a predefined RAG template and prefixed to the user's prompt, providing a more informed and contextually relevant response.\nL76: One of the key advantages of RAG is its ability to access and integrate information from a variety of sources, making it an ideal solution for complex conversational scenarios. For instance, when a user asks a question related to a specific document or web page, RAG can retrieve and incorporate the relevant information from that source into the chat response. RAG can also retrieve and incorporate information from multimedia sources like YouTube videos.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nSpecial Notice on find({\"ref_id\":\"turn74view1\",\"pattern\":\"May\"}); Total lines: 117\nL22: ## Read about Trint’s latest bug fixes, improvements and new features recently added to Trint across April, May and June 2026.\nL23: \nL24: 【5†Release Update - April 2026】\nL25: \nL26: 【6†Release Update - May 2026】\nL27: \nL28: 【7†Release Update - June 2026】\nL29: \nL30: ### Release Update - April 2026\nL31: \nL32: #### New ✨\nL33: ##### AI Highlights are here!\nL34: \nL35: AI Highlighting is now a part of the AI Assistant, there to source and define key moments so you don’t have to. It works in two modes – live and pre-recorded – meaning teams can get highlights in seconds, ready to turn into content.\nL36: \nL37: Recent improvements to the AI Highlights feature include:\nL38:   * The quotes produced from the feature are less frequent and more selective, using a prompt tuned by our VP of Product’s domain knowledge.\nL39:   * The tool is now triggered on the end of a sentence as well as at the end of paragraph.\nL40:   * The triggering logic has been optimised, so it is now triggering every 15 seconds, meaning highlights are set to be updated more often, avoiding long delays before the first highlight generated.\nL41: For more information about AI Highlights, please see our 【8†support guide here】 or speak to your Customer Success Manager.\nL42: ####\nL43: \nL44: #### Improved 👩‍💻\nL45: \nL46: ##### Trint ASR version 1.29 release\nL47: \nL48: We now have a build version of our ASR Batch image which is better at handling segments and less likely to miss chunks of transcripts.\nL49: ##### Desktop App now uses H264 codec for live streaming\nL50: \nL51: The Desktop App was using an old FLV1 video codec which produces high bitrate compared to the standard RTMP codec H264. The FLV1 codec also doesn't allow for constant bitrate and framerate control, whereas the H264 codec will have fixed framerate and will downscale the video to 480p.\nL52: #### Bugs 🐞\nL53:   * We fixed a bug in the transcript watcher API that prevented messages going to a dlq when errors occurred and also cleaned up the part of the code that caused the issue.\nL54:   * We’ve patched up the server that handles our RTMP push streams to clean up memory when a stream finishes.\nL55:   * We fixed a bug that could set some transcript states to null during secure deletions, which prevented unicorns UI from rendering properly, and stopped us from easily getting visibility of the outcome of the deletions.\nL56: * * *\nL57: ### Release Update - May 2026\nL58: \nL59: #### New ✨\nL60: ##### The Trint MCP Connector is live!\nL61: \nL62: Possibly our most exciting update for this quarter — the Trint MCP - OAuth Proxy is live in all environments. Our MCP lets external AI clients (Claude, ChatGPT, Cursor) safely call a Trint platform on a user's behalf over a standard protocol, allowing Trint to show up as a tool inside those assistants. It puts Trint where users already work — inside the AI assistants they use daily — so they can reach for Trint by reflex instead of context-switching.\nL63: 【9†Image】【10†Image】\nL64: \nL65: For more information about the Trint MCP Connector, please see our 【11†support guide here】 or speak to your Customer Success Manager.\nL66: ##### A new CI/CD pipeline\nL67: \nL68: This new pipeline enables automated releases of the Trint Desktop app for macOS, removing the dependency on properly configured Mac laptops and streamlining the process.\nL69: \nL70: ##### A new version of the Trint Desktop app launched\nL71: \nL72: Version (v0.1.21) of the Desktop app launched, with the release including a major Electron upgrade, from v34.5.6 to v41.6.0.\nL73: \nL74: #### Improved 👩‍💻\nL75: ##### Trint ASR version 1.31 and 1.32 release\nL76: \nL77: In version 1.30, we joined all consecutive segments with the same speaker then filtered out any segment shorter than the minimum segment duration. With release 1.31 we do this and then join consecutive segments with the same speaker. Version 1.32 trains a new version of the hallucinations detector model which has shown improved quality in hallucinations detection.\nL78: ##### Improved UI in the AI Highlights panel\nL79:   * We’ve updated the UI to streamline the panel design.\nL80:   * There is now a sticky header and footer section and users can see both the suggested and accepted suggestions in the panel for each session.\nL81:   * Footer shows the count for suggested and accepted highlights in a session and has the Accept all and Dismiss all buttons.\nL82:   * Highlighted text in the Editor improved so the start and end of a suggestion has borders and two consecutive suggestions have empty space in between helping users to visually separate highlights easily.\nL83: 【12†Image】\nL84: \nL85: 【13†Image】\nL86: #### Bugs 🐞\nL87: \nL88:   * Fixed a bug that affected subscriptions routed from the marketing site, via the buy now sign up flow.\nL89:   * We have fixed an issue in the Trint Desktop app where users were required to log in again every time they closed the app. The fix has been released in v.0.1.22.\nL90: \nL91: * * *\nL92: \nL93: ### Release Update - June 2026\nL94: \nL95: #### New ✨\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nHow to Set up the Trint MCP Connector (https://info.trint.com/knowledge/how-to-set-up-the-trint-mcp-connector)\nciteturn75view5 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Content type: text/html; Source: find({\"ref_id\":\"turn74view2\",\"pattern\":\"Gemini\"}); Total lines: 134\nL0: 【0†iframe†www.googletagmanager.com】\nL1: \nL2: 【1†Skip to content】\nL3: \nL4: English Show submenu for translations\nL5: \nL6: 【2†Customer portal】\nL7: \nL8: Open main navigation Close main navigation\nL9: \nL10:   * English Show submenu for translations\nL11: \nL12:   * 【3†Customer portal】\nL13:   * 【4†Go to trint.com†trint.com】\nL14: \nL15: 【4†Go to trint.com†trint.com】\nL16: \nL17: Looking to do more with Trint? Follow our step-by-step guide to connecting your AI agent to Trint.\nL23: \nL24: Most AI tools will give you a summary, pull some quotes, and move on. What they won't tell you is whether the quote is accurate, who actually said it, or where in the recording it came from.\nL25: \nL26: The Trint MCP Connector lets AI assistants like Claude and ChatGPT access your Trint account securely and safely.\nL27: An MCP (Model Context Protocol) server is an open standard that lets AI agents and assistants access external tools and data sources. The Trint MCP Connector uses this open standard, so that journalists, researchers, and media teams already working with Claude or another AI assistant, can bring Trint’s capabilities directly into their workflows, without switching context.\nL28: \nL29: The AI tool does the work. You will then see Trint as an option to connect. From here, select the Trint connector.\nL54: \nL55: 【12†Image: Claude MCP Setup step 4】\nL56: \nL57: Step 5: Click the ‘Connect’ button. You will then be prompted to provide authentication via the Trint app.\nL58: \nL59: 【13†Image: Claude MCP setup step 5】\nL60: \nL61: Step 6: Log into Trint as usual.\nL62: \nL63: 【14†Image】\nL64: \nL65: Step 7: Your Trint and Claude are now connected.\nL66: \nL67: 【15†Image】\nL68: Step 8: You will now be able to see Trint in your connectors page. From here you can set permissions for each tool and customize access.\nL69: \nL70: 【16†Image】\nL71: \nL72: Step 9: Once your permissions have been set, you can then toggle the Trint Connector on and off in the chat page.\nL73: \nL74: 【17†Image】\nL75: \nL76: Step 10: From here, you are ready to use the Connector. Verification and Fact-Checking Standards In matters of fairness there is one specific requirement: All producers must have a fact-checking procedure at the completion of the program. Pay attention to the proper spelling of names and titles, correct dates, accurate translation of foreign language material, etc. Producers should be prepared to show and cite their sourcing or sources in a fact-checking document or annotated script. Producers should generally review assertions of fact given by experts, especially if any credible questions arise about their accuracy. If an expert’s assertion calls into question the character or competency of another person or entity or is otherwise possibly defamatory, then the assertion may not be accepted without verification. All factual assertions by non-expert interviewees should also be checked. Nothing in the fairness rules should be interpreted as preventing a producer from making a point-of-view or authored program. No Byline Policy In cases where a story is attributed to ‘FRONTLINE,’ often seen on corporate or government statements responding to our reporting, or pages of collected works; the text was written by a member of our editorial staff and the journalism included sourced to the appropriate reporter or reporting teams. So, if you are doing all your work in a single tool or connected suite of tools, you can skip this entire section (apart from the relinking stage).\nL573: \nL574: If you do need to send your project from your NLE to another piece of software, however, you have a series of steps to follow.\nL575: \nL576:   1. 1\nL577: Export a reference video\nL578: \nL579:   2. 2\nL580: \nL581: Relink back to the original camera files\nL582: \nL583:   3. 3\nL584: \nL585: Simplify the timeline drastically\nL586: \nL587:   4. 4\nL588: \nL589: Export the timeline into a new format\nL590: \nL591:   5. 5\nL592: \nL593: Import into the finishing tool and check for errors\nL594: It’s unusual for one person to perform all of these steps, and the order may vary slightly depending on who is doing the conform. In some cases, the editorial team may do most of the conform, but it’s more common for most of the work to be done by the colorist or finishing artist who is receiving the project.\nL595: ### Export a Reference Video\nL596: \nL597: The first step in the conform is to export a reference video of the entire project as it was in its locked state. That is important because we’re about to do a whole bunch of moving, swapping, and exchanging files. The reference video provides a check later on to make sure that everything was transferred properly.\nL598: ## Relink back to the original camera files\nL599: \nL600: Share\nL601: \nL602: Most people these days use an offline editing workflow (and the ones that don’t, probably should), which means that they were editing off of lower-resolution proxy files.\nL603: Those proxy files are great for editing, but they’re very low quality compared to the original camera files, and the colorist and VFX teams need access to all of the data that was originally captured. Even if you’re doing all of your color and VFX work inside your NLE, you’re still going to relink back to those camera files before you export.\nL604: \nL605: 【107†Image】\nL606: Depending on what tool you’re using and how all of your files were named and stored, the process of relinking could be as simple as clicking one button. Or, it could be many long hours of manually comparing files, one at a time. If you’re consistent with your file naming, however, it can almost always be automated.\nL607: \nL608: 【63†Proxy workflows in FCP X】\nL609: \nL610: 【64†Proxy workflows in Premiere Pro】\nL611: \nL612: 【65†Proxy workflows in Avid】\nL613: \nL614: These three guides contributed by Sofi Marshall, 【66†Freelance Editor†www.imdb.com】.\nL615: 【67†Proxy workflows in Resolve】\nL616: \nL617: Contributed by Seth Goldin, Tech, post-production, and workflow at 【68†Freethink Media†www.freethink.com】.\nL618: If your sound was recorded separately from your video, you also have to relink your audio back to the original files from your audio recordist. When you created proxy files for the editors to use, the camera files and the audio files were merged. Each clip in the finished timeline thus has to be matched to two different original source files—one from the camera, and one from the sound recorder.\nL619: ### Simplify the timeline\nL620: \nL621: Most interchange formats unfortunately can’t handle much complexity in the timeline, and so we need to simplify the timeline down to the most [REDACTED_SECRET]—a series of clips, one after another—before we export.\nL622: Nearly all editors edit with a multi-track timeline, or else FCP X’s magnetic timeline. In either case, it’s common to have a dozen or more different layers of video. Imagine that the different video clips were stacked on top of each other. Whichever one is on top is the one that will be seen.\nL623: \nL624: 【108†Image】\nL625: \nL626: In order to prep for the conform, the film must be collapsed down to a single track.\nL627: \nL628: 【109†Image】\nL629: We also have to remove most (if not all) of the effects and transitions on our clips, since those effects and transitions will probably not translate to the software used for color and finishing. The finishing artist will have to recreate each one in the new software. This is another reason why it was important to export out a reference video, because the finishing artist will refer closely to that video file in order to recreate all of the effects and transitions that the editors used.\nL630: ## Export the timeline into a new format\nL631: \nL632: Share\nL633: \nL634: You would think that, by this point, we would have a universal format that everyone could use to exchange edit timelines between different software tools, but alas, one does n…555 tokens truncated…tools usually have the ability to auto-detect where cuts are and split the film up automatically. Those tools aren’t perfect, however, and so it’s still necessary to check the film closely and compare it to the reference video.\nL651: If you are using this method to send the film to another team, it’s essential that you relink to the camera files before this step, otherwise you’ll be sending a low-quality file.\nL652: \nL653: Also, it’s very important that you understand the codec, color space, and bit depth of the source material to ensure that no color information is lost during this export. All of the specifications of the exported video must be equal to or better than the source footage.\nL654: \nL655: 【111†Choosing a codec to send to color correction】\nL656: Premiere Pro has a useful tool called Smart Render that can come in handy at this stage, if you are using one of the compatible codecs. Instead of rendering and exporting the clips on the timeline, it copies the data straight from the source files into the export file, keeping the color information untouched.\nL657: \nL658: 【112†Smart Rendering】\nL659: If the camera files are in a raw format, then this method is not a good idea, since you’ll prevent the colorist from having access to the raw information. Once you’ve exported a raw file, you have lost some of the information inside it.\nL660: ## Import into the finishing tool and check for errors\nL661: \nL662: Share\nL663: \nL664: Once you’ve transferred the project to the colorist or finishing artist, they will import the project into their software. Depending on the type of workflow, the relinking process might happen at this stage, instead of earlier in the process.\nL665: Then the artist will also import the reference video that we made at the beginning of the conform, and place it on top of the video track with a blend mode set to Difference. The Difference blend mode will calculate the difference between the top track (the reference video) and the bottom track (the conformed video). Any pixels that are identical between the two tracks will appear black, while any pixels that are different will appear in color.\nL666: \nL667: 【113†Blend Modes】\nL668: The artist then watches the entire film through to confirm that nothing has gone wrong. It’s unlikely that the film will be entirely black, because there will be many minor differences between the reference video and the conformed version. The conformed version will have no color correction, and no temporary effects that the editorial team added. The difference mode will highlight any serious issues, like the film being out of sync, or missing or incorrect clips being inserted.\nL669: In spite of how careful everyone is to prevent these issues, it’s rare that everything just works the first time.\nL670: \nL671: 【114†The Most Common Conform Issues】\nL672: \nL673: Contributed by Matt Schneider, Director of Technology, 【115†Technicolor New York†www.postworks.com】.\nL674: \nL675: Share this chapter\nL676: ### Continue reading for free\nL677: \nL678: Unlock all 100,000 words of the Frame.io Workflow Guide and learn how the pros do workflow.\nL679: \nL680: [Input: Email address]\nL681: \nL682: Unlock\nL683: \nL684: 【116†Image】\nL685: \nL686: 05\nL687: \nL688: ##### Chapter 05\nL689: # VFX\nL690: \nL691: We all know that VFX creates new worlds, but it can also save a huge amount of time and money in this world (when done properly).\nL692: \nL693: 12 minute read\nL694: \nL695: 【117†Image】\nL696: \nL697: When you hear visual effects (VFX), you probably first think of monsters and spaceships. You’re not wrong, but as the tools have gotten cheaper and easier to use, VFX have become ubiquitous in motion pictures. Even a simple drama or corporate video project frequently makes use of VFX to fix small errors or save the production time and money.\nL698: 【118†Special Effects vs. Visual Effects】\nL699: ### Invisible Effects\nL700: \nL701: The vast majority of VFX that appear in modern films are “【119†invisible effects†blog.frame.io】,” which means that (ideally) the audience has no idea they’re watching something that’s either been digitally altered or is completely synthetic.\nL702: \nL703: Perhaps you have a scene in which a house catches fire. You could build a dummy shell of a house and actually set it on fire, or you could save a production time and money by adding computer-generated (CG) fire.\nL704: \nL705: 【120†Image】\nL706: The fire in “Get Out” was computer-generated.\nL707: \nL708: Or perhaps you recorded a killer CEO interview for your latest marketing video, but didn’t realize that the CEO was wearing a shirt with the Ralph Lauren Polo symbol. Since you’re probably not excited about contacting Ralph Lauren’s PR team, you’re going to need to use VFX to remove it.\nL709: Or maybe you’re shooting a period piece and want to remove modern “tells” such as signage or streetlights. Or you have an actor who has two perfectly functional legs but is supposed to look like a double amputee.\nL710: \nL711: Suffice it to say, since digital visual effects applications are now widely available, it’s more likely than not that any feature film (or high-end commercial or mainstream TV show) will use them to fix something that the viewer will never suspect as being inauthentic.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nRetrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) / Open WebUI (https://docs.openwebui.com/features/chat-conversations/rag/)\nciteturn76view4 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Content type: text/html; Source: find({\"ref_id\":\"turn75view1\",\"pattern\":\"citation\"}); Total lines: 428\nL120: Once created, the external source appears in Workspace > Knowledge like any other knowledge base and can be attached to models or chats. At chat time, Open WebUI embeds the user's query with its configured RAG embedding model, searches the external database by vector, and feeds the top matches into the prompt. No copy of the documents is stored in Open WebUI.\nL121: In the sources under an answer, each result is listed by its title. When a result's Source Field holds a web address, clicking its title or the Content link in the citation opens that page; otherwise the title is plain text.\nL122: \nL123: Embedding model must match\nL124: Because Open WebUI embeds the query and searches your database by vector, the vectors stored in your external database must come from the same embedding model Open WebUI uses (matching model and dimensions). Mismatched embeddings produce poor or meaningless results.\nL125: ## Web Search for RAG【50†​】\nL126: \nL127: warning\nL128: \nL129: Context Length Warning for Ollama Users: Web pages typically contain 4,000-8,000+ tokens even after content extraction, including main content, navigation elements, headers, footers, and metadata. With only 4096 tokens available, you're often getting half the page content, missing the most relevant information. Even 4096 tokens is frequently insufficient for comprehensive web content analysis.\nL190: ### Design decisions and why they matter【60†​】\nL191: \nL192: Forward-only merging: Small chunks always merge into the next chunk, never backward. This keeps the logic simple and predictable, and preserves the natural \"this section introduces what follows\" relationship common in documents. A brief intro section merging forward into the content it introduces makes semantic sense.\nL193: Why not backward merging? Beyond added code complexity, backward merging would frequently fail anyway. By the time any chunk gets finalized, it's in one of two states: either it grew to meet or exceed `CHUNK_MIN_SIZE_TARGET` through absorption (so it's already \"satisfied\" with limited headroom), or it couldn't absorb the next chunk because that would exceed `CHUNK_SIZE` (so it's already bumping against the ceiling).\nL194: Either way, a backward merge attempt would often fail the size check, meaning you'd add branching logic and state tracking for something that rarely succeeds.\nL195: No cross-document merging: Chunks from different source files are never combined, even if both are small. This preserves clear document boundaries for citation, source attribution, and retrieval context.\nL196: \nL197: Respects maximum size: If merging two chunks would exceed `CHUNK_SIZE`, both are kept separate. Content is never discarded to force a merge.\nL198: Metadata inheritance: Merged chunks inherit metadata from the first chunk in the merge sequence. This is consistent with forward-merge semantics: source and header information reflects where the merged section \"started,\" which is typically the right choice for retrieval and citation purposes.\nL199: The `\\n\\n` separator: When chunks merge, they're joined with double newlines rather than concatenated directly. This preserves visual and structural separation in the combined text, which can matter for both embedding quality and human readability if you inspect your chunks.\nL200: ### Edge cases【61†​】\nL201: \nL202: Consecutive tiny chunks: Handled naturally. They keep accumulating into a single chunk until the threshold is met or max size would be exceeded.\nL203: Small chunk followed by large chunk: If a small chunk is followed by a chunk large enough that merging would exceed `CHUNK_SIZE`, the small chunk gets finalized as-is, still undersized. This is unavoidable without backward merging or content splitting, but it's also rare in practice. It typically occurs at natural semantic boundaries (a brief transition before a dense section), and the small chunk being standalone at that boundary is arguably correct anyway.\nL204: Last chunk in document: If the final chunk is undersized, it stays undersized since there's nothing to merge forward into. Again, unavoidable and usually fine: document endings are natural boundaries.\nL205: ### Performance characteristics【62†​】\nL206: \nL207: The algorithm is O(n) in the number of chunks: a single pass with no lookahead or backtracking. This makes it fast even for large document collections.\nL208: The efficiency gains from merging scale non-linearly in some ways. Retrieval over 45 vectors versus 588 isn't just ~13x faster in raw compute, you're also getting much cleaner top-k results because you've eliminated the noise of near-empty chunks that might score well on partial keyword matches but contribute nothing useful to the LLM. The quality improvement often matters more than the speed improvement.\nL209: Testing has shown that a well-configured threshold (e.g., 1000 for a chunk size of 2000) can reduce chunk counts by over 90% while improving retrieval accuracy, because each remaining chunk carries meaningful semantic context rather than being a fragment that confuses both the embedding model and the retrieval ranking.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nAI-Powered Media Asset Management | Axle AI (https://www.axle.ai/)\nciteturn76view5 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; [Input: Search for answers] [Input] [Input]\nL18: \nL19:   * There are no suggestions because the search field is empty.\nL20: \nL21: # Release Update - Summer 2026\nL22: ## Read about Trint’s latest bug fixes, improvements and new features recently added to Trint across April, May and June 2026.\nL23: \nL24: 【5†Release Update - April 2026】\nL25: \nL26: 【6†Release Update - May 2026】\nL27: \nL28: 【7†Release Update - June 2026】\nL29: \nL30: ### Release Update - April 2026\nL31: \nL32: #### New ✨\nL33: ##### AI Highlights are here!\nL34: \nL35: AI Highlighting is now a part of the AI Assistant, there to source and define key moments so you don’t have to. It works in two modes – live and pre-recorded – meaning teams can get highlights in seconds, ready to turn into content.\nL36: \nL37: Recent improvements to the AI Highlights feature include:\nL38:   * The quotes produced from the feature are less frequent and more selective, using a prompt tuned by our VP of Product’s domain knowledge.\nL39:   * The tool is now triggered on the end of a sentence as well as at the end of paragraph.\nL40:   * The triggering logic has been optimised, so it is now triggering every 15 seconds, meaning highlights are set to be updated more often, avoiding long delays before the first highlight generated.\nL41: For more information about AI Highlights, please see our 【8†support guide here】 or speak to your Customer Success Manager.\nL42: ####\nL43: \nL44: #### Improved 👩‍💻\nL45: \nL46: ##### Trint ASR version 1.29 release\nL47: \nL48: We now have a build version of our ASR Batch image which is better at handling segments and less likely to miss chunks of transcripts.\nL49: ##### Desktop App now uses H264 codec for live streaming\nL50: \nL51: The Desktop App was using an old FLV1 video codec which produces high bitrate compared to the standard RTMP codec H264. The FLV1 codec also doesn't allow for constant bitrate and framerate control, whereas the H264 codec will have fixed framerate and will downscale the video to 480p.\nL52: #### Bugs 🐞\nL53:   * We fixed a bug in the transcript watcher API that prevented messages going to a dlq when errors occurred and also cleaned up the part of the code that caused the issue.\nL54:   * We’ve patched up the server that handles our RTMP push streams to clean up memory when a stream finishes.\nL55:   * We fixed a bug that could set some transcript states to null during secure deletions, which prevented unicorns UI from rendering properly, and stopped us from easily getting visibility of the outcome of the deletions.\nL56: * * *\nL57: ### Release Update - May 2026\nL58: \nL59: #### New ✨\nL60: ##### The Trint MCP Connector is live!\nL61: \nL62: Possibly our most exciting update for this quarter — the Trint MCP - OAuth Proxy is live in all environments. Our MCP lets external AI clients (Claude, ChatGPT, Cursor) safely call a Trint platform on a user's behalf over a standard protocol, allowing Trint to show up as a tool inside those assistants. It puts Trint where users already work — inside the AI assistants they use daily — so they can reach for Trint by reflex instead of context-switching.\nL63: 【9†Image】【10†Image】\nL64: \nL65: For more information about the Trint MCP Connector, please see our 【11†support guide here】 or speak to your Customer Success Manager.\nL66: ##### A new CI/CD pipeline\nL67: \nL68: This new pipeline enables automated releases of the Trint Desktop app for macOS, removing the dependency on properly configured Mac laptops and streamlining the process.\nL69: \nL70: ##### A new version of the Trint Desktop app launched\nL71: \nL72: Version (v0.1.21) of the Desktop app launched, with the release including a major Electron upgrade, from v34.5.6 to v41.6.0.\nL73: \nL74: #### Improved 👩‍💻\nL75: ##### Trint ASR version 1.31 and 1.32 release\nL76: \nL77: In version 1.30, we joined all consecutive segments with the same speaker then filtered out any segment shorter than the minimum segment duration. With release 1.31 we do this and then join consecutive segments with the same speaker. Version 1.32 trains a new version of the hallucinations detector model which has shown improved quality in hallucinations detection.\nL78: ##### Improved UI in the AI Highlights panel\nL79:   * We’ve updated the UI to streamline the panel design.\nL80:   * There is now a sticky header and footer section and users can see both the suggested and accepted suggestions in the panel for each session.\nL81:   * Footer shows the count for suggested and accepted highlights in a session and has the Accept all and Dismiss all buttons.\nL82:   * Highlighted text in the Editor improved so the start and end of a suggestion has borders and two consecutive suggestions have empty space in between helping users to visually separate highlights easily.\nL83: 【12†Image】\nL84: \nL85: 【13†Image】\nL86: #### Bugs 🐞\nL87: \nL88:   * Fixed a bug that affected subscriptions routed from the marketing site, via the buy now sign up flow.\nL89:   * We have fixed an issue in the Trint Desktop app where users were required to log in again every time they closed the app. The fix has been released in v.0.1.22.\nL90: \nL91: * * *\nL92: \nL93: ### Release Update - June 2026\nL94: \nL95: #### New ✨\nL96: ##### Quote player tool in the Trint MCP Connector\nL97: \nL98: This tool has now been shipped in the Trint MCP Connector which should provide a richer experience in Agents along with a standardised way to render UI within our own internal agent.\nL99: \nL100: #### Improved 👩‍💻\nL101: \nL102: ##### Increased resolution of video previews\nL103: \nL104: The resolution of the video previews for Realtime Trints has been increased from 320px to 480px wide, bringing it up to parity with batch uploads. This means that previews now render noticeably sharper.\nL105: ##### SSO email login improvements\nL106: \nL107: We can now exempt specific email addresses from SSO on an SSO-enforced org, so nominated accounts keep username and password login while HRD is enabled for the rest of the organisation.\nL108: ##### Updates to the waveform generation for Realtime\nL109: \nL110: We’ve made changes to the way we generate the waveform for Realtime. Previously the waveform was generated by our general cluster in AWS Batch and we now use Lambdas container.\nL111: \nL112: trint.com Support Hub\nL113: \nL114: Copyright © 2026, Trint\nL115: \nL116: 【0†iframe†www.googletagmanager.com】\n"}]

What is already solved? | **FACT:** Frame.io V4 supports version history and different-type side-by-side comparison; Axle keeps existing storage [C11–C12,C17]. Native conform/QC relies on specialist media tools [C10]. Version mismatch alone is not a new opportunity. |\\n+| 8. Same files to general AI? | **INFERENCE:** For readable brief/approval/export packets, yes, plausible substitution. Native timeline/pixel/audio QC is outside the public Skill boundary; the competitor's ability to do it is a reason to downgrade us. |\\n+| 9. NAS versus alternatives? | **INFERENCE:** Large concurrent production originals and existing remote replication can make NAS architecture useful. Drive/SharePoint can still own briefs/reviews. A mounted NAS plus existing MAM/AI retains originals and erases most claimed access distinction. |\\n+| 10. Three-minute demonstration? | **INFERENCE:** A wrong-version visual is instantly understandable but very familiar. A technically authentic conform demo needs unsupported parsing/rendering. No additional Hero proposed. |\\n+\\n+**Reason not retained:** Best production-storage evidence does not equal best Evidence-first fit. Standard version/correspondence jobs have especially strong incumbent tools; original-media QC exceeds the allowed runtime.\\n+\\n+## 4. What competitors already solve — fair baseline\\n+\\n+FACT here denotes published capability, not hands-on verification. Absence of an exact contract in a marketing page is **UNKNOWN**, not a product gap. No paid-plan or upload-limit handicap in comparisons.\\n+\\n+| Competitor | Already documented | Remaining claim we can/cannot make |\\n+|---|---|---|\\n+| DAM / MAM, specifically Axle [C17] | Existing NAS/SAN/folder cataloging, proxies, transcripts/metadata/search; media review/collaboration. On-prem/private/cloud options. | Cannot claim ownership of persistent media context or non-migration. Exact arbitrary source-document claim review UNKNOWN. |\\n+| Trint [C13–C16] | Transcript/audio review, source timecodes, story assembly/editor handoff; MCP retrieves/searches transcripts, plays quotes with source mapping and exports inside AI. May 2026 connector and June quote-player release documented. | Strongest incumbent for the selected factual-review job. External PDFs/archive indexes plus final-script changes are a plausible boundary, not a proven missing feature. |\\n+| Frame.io V4 [C11–C12] | Version stacks/history and comparison of assets including different supported types, with comments. Current V4 differs from older same-type legacy limits. | Cannot sell simple version comparison. Cross-source editorial sufficiency remains a human job; need not imply absent AI integration. |\\n+| UGREEN [C18,C22] | Universal search, file chat, knowledge/document summaries, image OCR/recognition and voice-related features. | Public marketing is not a guarantee the exact review contract exists or is absent. Actual model route must be verified separately. |\\n+| Synology [C19] | AI Console supports document-context search, OCR/STT/image captions on supported GPU systems; third-party integrations and administration. | Not a photo-only/keyword-only baseline. Its runtime capabilities do not transfer to UGREEN Skill actions. |\\n+| QNAP Qsirch [C20] | Semantic search/analytical RAG; distinct GPU-dependent AI Mode advertises local multimodal search/summaries. | Not merely a list of search hits. Exact exhaustive claim coverage/current-version behavior UNKNOWN. |\\n+| ChatGPT local projects [C23] | Official docs distinguish uploaded/connected project context and folder-attached local projects; files can be read/changed in attached roots. | Assume a suitable available workspace/permissions; not every ordinary uploaded project equals folder access, and local access is not offline inference. |\\n+| Claude Cowork [C24] | Desktop-mediated local read/write; cloud execution and persistent workspaces. Trint separately documents Claude integration. | No universal repeated-upload claim. Actual mounted-share behavior needs validation, not an assumed blocker. |\\n+| Gemini [C25] | Documents/images/audio/video and Drive inputs; same bounded corpus is a strong baseline. | Trint's Gemini setup is still “coming soon” in the read guide [C16]; do not claim every connector route is live. Do not infer inability to reason over supplied content. |\\n+| Citation RAG / Open WebUI [C21] | Local/remote context retrieval and citations/source metadata. | Conflict reasoning can be prompted or tooled. Source coverage, version ledger and saved human disposition are workflow controls that can be added; no new architecture established. |\\n+\\n+## 5. Three strongest objections\\n+\\n+### Objection 1 — “Just give the same files to ChatGPT?”\\n+\\n+**Answer: for the bounded content reasoning, probably yes.** This is an INFERENCE, not a run result. ChatGPT/Claude/Gemini with the same authorized script, transcripts, PDFs, index and image can plausibly extract claims, compare source statements, identify qualification/conflict and report uncertainty. Give the baseline the same source-authority instructions, prior report, read-state requirements and save destination.\\n+\\n+The stronger baseline is **same-folder AI plus the user's already configured Trint/MAM**, not a blank chat with only a few selected snippets. Trint's connected-source tools can remove reattachment and quote-location burden [C14–C16]. Tool-enabled general AI can also inventory inputs, hash files and save reports; do not reserve these for SourceWeave.\\n+\\n+**Only plausible residual, ASSUMPTION:** Repeatable preparation/current-version/source-identity checking remains awkward at a documented boundary across the existing systems. Packaging could help an existing NAS owner if it reduces that observed work. No evidence of measured savings, unique access or higher accuracy exists here. Identical convenience/quality from the baseline is a kill result.\\n+\\n+### Objection 2 — “Isn't this RAG + citation + conflict labels?”\\n+\\n+**Answer: that description can be substantially correct.** Retrieval does not prohibit contradiction reasoning [C21]. Four labels are a presentation choice, not intellectual novelty. The proposed contract would additionally record authorized input identities, per-file read/partial/failure states, dated/versioned observations and human unresolved decisions. Those are useful controls only if reliably implemented and valued; ordinary RAG/agents can add them too.\\n+\\n+Strongest possible differentiator is **a repeatable review after a script/source change with explicit scope and evidence coverage**, not semantic search or a “truth score.” It is currently a product/workflow hypothesis. If the demo only changes the color of a conflict row, the objection wins. No automatic dependency graph, selective incremental recomputation or guaranteed exhaustive reading is claimed.\\n+\\n+### Objection 3 — “Why must this need NAS?”\\n+\\n+**Answer: it does not inherently require NAS.** A packet copied to Drive/Dropbox/SharePoint preserves its semantics. For a cloud-native checking workflow, those may be the better home. NAS adds operational fit only when that team already repeatedly uses shared long-lived originals and local project history; no migration is then required. Particular production cases establish that architecture, not general superiority.\\n+\\n+| Removal/substitution | What may be lost | What is retained / result |\\n+|---|---|---|\\n+| Replace production NAS with Drive/SharePoint | Existing high-volume concurrent-original editing/ingest topology may need migration; actual cost UNKNOWN. | Small checking packet, source links and governed reviews can work there. No necessity established. |\\n+| Use Dropbox/Drive already holding checking sources | Nothing NAS-specific demonstrated for the factual job; IDA's real workflow already does this. | Claim checking remains possible. Do not require NAS copies artificially. |\\n+| Use an equivalent shared file server | No relevant architectural loss if team/local-original access is equivalent. | N2 can qualify; this is not NAS-protocol exclusivity. |\\n+| Mount the existing NAS into file-aware AI | NAS is not removed at all. | Original location, team storage and save destination retained; this directly tests replacing our Skill. Strongest substitution risk. |\\n+| Keep NAS plus Axle/Trint/Frame.io | Existing MAM/connected transcript/review infrastructure remains. | Must prove a specific residual after their correct use; no PASS demonstrated. |\\n+\\n+**Privacy boundary, FACT/UNKNOWN:** UGREEN's notice permits optional third-party model transfers [C22]. NAS storage does not imply local inference, privacy, effective ACL isolation or zero exfiltration. Actual provider path and user policy would need separate verification. We did not inspect or configure them.\\n+\\n+## 6. Exactly one recommended Hero Scenario\\n+\\n+### Hero — Documentary pre-lock source review after a script change\\n+\\n+This is the only Hero recommendation. It is a **conditional research target under REVISE**, not a validated launch concept. No runner-up or additional demo scenario is recommended.\\n+\\n+| Required definition | Bounded definition |\\n+|---|---|\\n+| Target user | Producer/researcher on a small factual documentary team already using NAS/shared local originals, who personally maintains a source-backed checking script and receives revisions across tools. Not every Creator or every NAS owner. |\\n+| Real workflow | After narration/source changes, before editorial lock, check which revised assertions are supported, qualified or disputed by the identified sources and whether declared archive assets correspond. Human confirms current script/source authority. Completion-stage/source checking is evidenced [C01–C02]; this exact NAS handoff and unmet burden remain ASSUMPTION. |\\n+| NAS-native advantage | Existing shared source media/documents can be revisited beside their retained originals and prior review record without migrating the production archive. Conditional architectural convenience; no unique advantage over mounted-folder AI established. |\\n+| Strongest incumbent | Trint, especially MCP/source-linked quote playback, with Axle and Frame.io as the existing storage/review stack. No claim the combined stack is incapable of this job. |\\n+| Residual gap | ASSUMPTION: final-script changes require repeated cross-checks against external PDFs, transcript exports and archive identities that remain awkward across properly operated systems. Whether this exists is the largest unknown. |\\n+| Strongest general-AI risk | A file-aware ChatGPT/Claude workspace reading the same NAS root, plus Trint where already authorized, reproduces both reasoning and preparation/report persistence. High substitution risk. |\\n+| One sentence | Before locking a revised documentary script, check whether its assertions and declared archive assets still correspond to the identified source versions, and show unread inputs and unresolved conflicts before the editor decides. |\\n+| Biggest kill gate | A real reviewer using their incumbent correctly and same-folder AI has no material recurring preparation/source-verification burden left. If so, drop the NAS-specific direction even if the synthetic example looks impressive. |\\n+\\n+### Proposed <3-minute diagnostic demonstration — script only, no artifacts created\\n+\\n+**Audience message:** “This line was checked yesterday. The script changed. Does the evidence still support today's version?” Domain-neutral synthetic subject, such as a town's transport pilot; no real allegation or confidential source.\\n+\\n+Exactly **10 planned input files**:\\n+\\n+1. `current_script.md` — current draft, named by the human as the review target, containing three claims and one archive caption.\\n+2. `prior_script.md` — prior checked wording; never selected as current just because its filename looks final.\\n+3. `prior_review.md` — explicitly synthetic prior observations with scope/version references; not represented as an actual completed run.\\n+4. `interview_transcript.txt` — provided transcript with speaker/time references; derivative whose original-audio reading is not asserted.\\n+5. `source_notice_old.pdf` — earlier notice; its continued existence does not make it current authority.\\n+6. `source_correction.pdf` — explicit dated correction/qualification, source-owner relationship declared in the synthetic packet. Newer date alone is insufficient authority.\\n+7. `archive_index.csv` — declared archival ID/caption/source-file correspondence, not automatic proof of image authenticity.\\n+8. `selected_still.jpg` — readable supplied archive ID/context, reviewed as a derivative still; no inference of location/authenticity from appearance alone.\\n+9. `clean_source.pdf` — supports a separate clean claim without disagreement in the supplied readable scope.\\n+10. `appendix_unread.pdf` — deliberately unreadable test input; actual failed/partial tool result must be exposed, not invented.\\n+\\n+**Planted issue:** The revised narration broadens “a pilot on one route” into “the first town-wide service.” A source correction and interview qualifier do not support that broadening. A prior checked script is not approval of the changed claim. A separate archive-caption/ID discrepancy tests declared correspondence. The clean claim remains supported within scope; an appendix-dependent claim is **UNCERTAIN**, not “no evidence exists.”\\n+\\n+| Planned time | Visible action / human meaning |\\n+|---|---|\\n+| 0–20 sec | Show changed narration versus prior wording and ask whether it remains supported. Human identifies current draft and authorized scope. |\\n+| 20–45 sec | Enumerate ten planned inputs, actual identities/read outcomes and prior-review applicability. Do not claim whole-NAS or full-video coverage. |\\n+| 45–95 sec | Open exact correction/interview passages; explain why broader wording lacks support or is challenged, without deciding historical truth. |\\n+| 95–125 sec | Show declared archive-ID/caption discrepancy and actual referenced index/image region if supported. No image authenticity judgment. |\\n+| 125–145 sec | Show clean control and unread appendix separately. The unread record could change a conclusion. |\\n+| 145–165 sec | Editor defers the disputed wording/asset and requests verification; approves only saving a new review note beside the packet. |\\n+| 165–175 sec | Show that today's target/source observations differ from the prior review; prior clearance is not silently reused. This is a manual invocation, not an event watcher or automatic invalidation engine. |\\n+\\n+**Total planned: 175 seconds.** Timing/latency/accuracy untested; this is not a claim it runs on a device. A single readable final-script/source example can be understood quickly, but a small synthetic packet alone proves neither NAS scale nor user value. A later authorized fair comparison must give general AI all ten files and the same prior context. If it yields the same useful workflow with no additional burden, the demonstration supports substitution, not differentiation.\\n+\\n+### Feasibility boundary — reuse prior public-capability audit\\n+\\n+Official kit **0.7.3**, catalog **2.0.0**, generated **2026-09-01**, previously pinned archive SHA-256 `cf557e5a022e4297892f39768132855154232efdedeb726b2845450ccd70e1b3` [C26]. No remapping or device execution this round.\\n+\\n+**FACT from prior audit:** Listing/stat/read/hash actions, document extract/QA/reconcile with repeated files, image OCR/VLM and approved text saving make a bounded text/PDF/JPEG packet plausible. **UNKNOWN:** Full read coverage, truncated-page handling, exact locators, CSV extraction behavior, cross-agent IDs, source-version observations and actual model route. Hashes identify bytes, not authenticity, authority or complete reading. Approval to save a note is not editorial clearance. Verify actual save output/path/content state rather than claiming success from a request.\\n+\\n+No arbitrary runtime code, Python/Docker, external HTTP/Trint connection, live MAM synchronization, scheduler, file events, notifications, ACL enforcement, video frame extraction, NLE parsing, waveform comparison, rendering or automatic publish/blocking is assumed. Prepared transcripts and stills are explicit derivatives; no full recording/final render review claimed. If the real useful job requires those excluded operations, this reduced packet is not a valid substitute for it.\\n+\\n+## 7. Decision gates and stop state\\n+\\n+These are proposed falsification criteria, not observed experiment results or User-approved thresholds.\\n+\\n+| Gate | What would justify advancing / what kills it |\\n+|---|---|\\n+| Real cohort | Must identify a reviewer whose checking authority genuinely relates to persistent NAS/shared originals. Media backup plus cloud-only fact checking is insufficient. |\\n+| Recurrent residual | Must observe repeated source/version reconstruction after correct incumbent use. A setup/import/training fix that removes it defeats the hypothesis. |\\n+| Fair AI baseline | Give same roots/files/prior context and strong instructions; allow existing Trint/MAM integrations. Equivalent burden/quality kills the claimed NAS-specific advantage. |\\n+| Coverage honesty | UGREEN must expose failed/partial/unread inputs and real source references. Silent omission or fabricated locator is a blocker. |\\n+| Changed-source honesty | No automatic freshness/clearance claim from filenames, timestamps or hashes alone. Human confirms authority; mutable sources require qualified observations. |\\n+| Public runtime | Essential video/timeline/audio/rights parsing outside available actions kills the proposed scope. Do not hide preprocessing as a Skill capability. |\\n+| Demo substance | If only OCR plus a table/conflict color remains, do not call it a differentiated NAS workflow. |\\n+| Consequential claims | No autonomous truth, defamation, permission, copyright, authenticity or publication clearance decisions. Failure to keep these human-controlled kills the review contract. |\\n+\\n+**Research complete; stop here.** If the User later authorizes further work, validate this one Hero with a real representative workflow and a correctly configured baseline; do not reopen four more topics or automatically build a proof. No contact, customer-data access, model subscription or next-stage task was initiated.\\n+\\n+## 8. Source log\\n+\\n+All accessed **2026-11-01**. Dates are source dates where available, not crawler dates. Primary official/practitioner sources preferred. Axle works over existing NAS/SAN [C17]. Frame.io V4 already compares different asset types and versions [C11–C12]. We cannot differentiate on “source-linked,” “no repeated upload,” “human in the loop” or “local originals” alone.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Why revise rather than continue the broad pitch:** Restrict the promise to an explicitly scoped final-script review after a change, across the owner's already-held source documents, transcripts and declared archive identities.
